DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player guides an auto‑advancing dot through a three‑lane tunnel, tapping to rotate the dot to the next lane (top→middle→bottom→top) as obstacles with lane‑specific gaps rush forward. Precise timing and pattern recognition make each pass feel rewarding.

## Player
- Avatar: a single neon dot that constantly moves forward down the tunnel.
- Controls: a tap or click (or key press) cycles the dot to the next lane in a fixed order.
- No other actions; the player only changes lanes.

## Core loop
1. The tunnel scrolls forward, bringing a new obstacle toward the dot every 1–3 seconds.
2. The player watches the upcoming obstacle’s gap layout.
3. Within the brief window before the obstacle reaches the dot, the player decides to stay or tap to shift lanes.
4. If the dot aligns with the gap, it passes safely; otherwise a collision ends the run.
5. The cycle repeats, with obstacle speed and gap patterns gradually becoming more challenging.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Lane‑gap walls** – solid barriers that fill two of the three lanes, leaving a single lane open. Their gap position changes each obstacle, forcing a lane change or hold.
- **Multi‑wall sequences** – short runs of consecutive walls where the open lane shifts unpredictably, testing rapid decision‑making.
- Optional visual flare: brief “spike” segments that briefly appear in a lane before the wall, adding visual telegraphing.

## Score
- Earn one point each time the dot safely traverses a wall’s gap.
- Bonus points may be awarded for streaks of consecutive passes without a lane change, encouraging risk‑taking.

## Win and lose
- **Lose:** Colliding with any solid part of a wall triggers a game‑over state, displaying the final score and a restart prompt.
- **Win:** After clearing a predetermined number of walls (e.g., 30) the game declares victory, showing a celebratory overlay and the final score.

## Feel
Fast‑paced, reflex‑driven arcade action with a sleek neon‑on‑dark aesthetic. The tunnel pulses with subtle gradients while the dot’s movement feels immediate. Simple chiptune blips accompany lane switches and successful passes, creating a rhythmic loop of tension and release.

## Why it's fun
The single‑tap lane‑cycle mechanic turns every obstacle into a quick, high‑stakes decision, delivering a satisfying “just‑in‑time” thrill with each near‑miss.

TASKS.md

show
1. Scaffold HTML and meta.json — create `index.html` with header, canvas, restart button and matching `meta.json` per CONVENTIONS.md.
2. Define game state objects — declare player, hazards array, score, and phase objects at the top of the script.
3. Implement main loop — add `startLoop` using `requestAnimationFrame`, with cancel logic, clearing canvas, calling `update(dt)` and `render()`.
4. Set up input handling — create a `keys` Set for keydown/keyup events, add R‑key restart hook and restart button click invoking a common reset function.
5. Add player movement — implement dt‑based lane‑cycling physics responding to input, updating player position each frame.
6. Create hazard system — define hazard data structure, spawn rules respecting `SAFE_RADIUS`, and move hazards each frame.
7. Add collision detection — detect player‑hazard collisions, transition to `gameover` state and stop the active loop.
8. Implement scoring — increase score on hazard passes, draw the score at top‑left each frame.
9. Render game elements — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, the player (high‑contrast dot), and HUD.
10. Show game‑over overlay — dim the background and display "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions.
11. Expose observable contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with state, score, player coordinates, visibility, and a short objective string.
12. Add audio helpers — lazy‑initialize an `AudioContext` and provide a `beep()` function for hit, score, and death events, wrapped in try/catch.

  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CANVAS_WIDTH = 800;
  const CANVAS_HEIGHT = 600;
  const LANE_COUNT = 3;
  const LANE_HEIGHT = CANVAS_HEIGHT / LANE_COUNT;
  const PLAYER_SPEED = 200;

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
  const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
  let loopId = null;
  let phase = 'menu'; // 'menu' | 'playing' | 'gameover'
  let score = 0;
  let player = { x: 0, y: 0, lane: 0, visible: true };
  let hazards = [];
  let audioCtx = null;

  // === RESET ==============================================================
  function reset() {
    // Reset basic state variables
    phase = 'playing';
    score = 0;
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  // === LOOP ==============================================================
  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        // Placeholder: update logic would go here
        // Placeholder: render logic would go here
        publishState();
      } catch (e) {
        console.error('loop error', e);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

// === INPUT ==============================================================
   const keys = new Set();
   document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
     keys.add(ev.key);
     if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') { reset(); }
     // Game-specific input will be added later
   });
   document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
     keys.delete(ev.key);
   });
   restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
   // Also allow clicking on canvas to cycle lane
   canvas.addEventListener('click', () => {
     // cycle lane
     player.lane = (player.lane + 1) % LANE_COUNT;
   });

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,
      score: score,
      player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: player.visible },
      objective: 'Tap to cycle lanes and avoid obstacles.',
    };
  }

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = 'square';
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain);
      gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start();
      osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch (e) {
      // ignore audio errors (e.g., autoplay restrictions)
    }
  }

  // === BOOT ==============================================================
  // Start the game automatically
  reset();
  </script>
</body>

Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. function update(dt) {
   // move player forward
   player.y += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;

```

And // === RENDER ==============================================================
function render() {
   // clear background
   ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
   ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_WIDTH, CANVAS_HEIGHT);
   // draw player
   const laneX = (player.lane + 0.5) * (CANVAS_WIDTH / LANE_COUNT);
   // using lane for x position; we could derive laneX.
   const px = laneX;
   const py = player.y;
   ctx.fillStyle = '#ff00aa'; // neon pink
   ctx.beginPath();
   ctx.arc(px, py, 8, 0, Math.PI*2);
   ctx.fill();

   // draw score
   ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
   ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
   ctx.textAlign = 'left';
   ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
}
```

- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).

prompt (12,517 chars)
## Reference example: "snake"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Snake",
  "description": "Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or WASD to turn",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "snake", "grid"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Snake</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; image-rendering: pixelated; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Snake</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or WASD to turn</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CELL = 25;
  const COLS = 32;            // 32 * 25 = 800
  const ROWS = 24;            // 24 * 25 = 600
  const TICK_START_MS = 140;  // snake step interval at game start
  const TICK_MIN_MS = 60;     // floor speed
  const TICK_SPEEDUP = 3;     // ms shaved off per food eaten

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let snake;          // array of { x, y } in grid coords; snake[0] is head
  let dir;            // { x, y } unit vector
  let nextDir;        // queued direction (so quick double-taps don't U-turn)
  let food;           // { x, y } in grid coords
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let stepIntervalMs;
  let stepAccumulatorMs;
  let phase;          // "playing" | "gameover"
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    snake = [{ x: 10, y: 12 }, { x: 9, y: 12 }, { x: 8, y: 12 }];
    dir = { x: 1, y: 0 };
    nextDir = dir;
    food = randomFreeCell();
    score = 0;
    stepIntervalMs = TICK_START_MS;
    stepAccumulatorMs = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    stepAccumulatorMs += dt * 1000;
    while (stepAccumulatorMs >= stepIntervalMs) {
      stepAccumulatorMs -= stepIntervalMs;
      step();
      if (phase !== "playing") return;
    }
  }

  function step() {
    // Commit queued direction; reject reversals.
    if (nextDir.x !== -dir.x || nextDir.y !== -dir.y) dir = nextDir;

    const head = snake[0];
    const nx = head.x + dir.x;
    const ny = head.y + dir.y;

    // Wall collision -> game over.
    if (nx < 0 || nx >= COLS || ny < 0 || ny >= ROWS) {
      gameOver();
      return;
    }
    // Self collision (skip last segment because it's about to move).
    for (let i = 0; i < snake.length - 1; i++) {
      if (snake[i].x === nx && snake[i].y === ny) {
        gameOver();
        return;
      }
    }

    snake.unshift({ x: nx, y: ny });

    if (nx === food.x && ny === food.y) {
      score++;
      stepIntervalMs = Math.max(TICK_MIN_MS, stepIntervalMs - TICK_SPEEDUP);
      food = randomFreeCell();
      beep(660, 0.06);
    } else {
      snake.pop();
    }
  }

  function gameOver() {
    phase = "gameover";
    if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
    beep(120, 0.18);
  }

  function randomFreeCell() {
    while (true) {
      const c = { x: Math.floor(Math.random() * COLS), y: Math.floor(Math.random() * ROWS) };
      if (!snake.some((s) => s.x === c.x && s.y === c.y)) return c;
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    // Background grid.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "rgba(125,249,255,0.04)";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1;
    for (let x = 0; x <= COLS; x++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(x * CELL, 0); ctx.lineTo(x * CELL, ROWS * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    for (let y = 0; y <= ROWS; y++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(0, y * CELL); ctx.lineTo(COLS * CELL, y * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }

    // Food (pulsing).
    const pulse = 0.85 + 0.15 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 180);
    drawCell(food.x, food.y, `rgba(255,82,174,${pulse.toFixed(3)})`);

    // Snake body.
    for (let i = snake.length - 1; i >= 1; i--) {
      drawCell(snake[i].x, snake[i].y, "#52ffae");
    }
    // Snake head — brighter so the player tracks it instantly.
    drawCell(snake[0].x, snake[0].y, "#9dffd1");

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}` + (score === highScore && score > 0 ? " — new best!" : ""), canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
    }
  }

  function drawCell(cx, cy, fill) {
    ctx.fillStyle = fill;
    ctx.fillRect(cx * CELL + 1, cy * CELL + 1, CELL - 2, CELL - 2);
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    switch (ev.key) {
      case "ArrowUp": case "w": case "W": nextDir = { x: 0, y: -1 }; break;
      case "ArrowDown": case "s": case "S": nextDir = { x: 0, y: 1 }; break;
      case "ArrowLeft": case "a": case "A": nextDir = { x: -1, y: 0 }; break;
      case "ArrowRight": case "d": case "D": nextDir = { x: 1, y: 0 }; break;
    }
  });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    const head = snake[0];
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                     // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: head.x * CELL + CELL / 2,    // canvas-space center
        y: head.y * CELL + CELL / 2,
        visible: head.x >= 0 && head.x < COLS && head.y >= 0 && head.y < ROWS,
      },
      objective: "Eat the pink food. Don't hit the walls or your own tail.",
    };
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. Failure feeds the error report back to you for correction.\n185: \n186: 1. Both `index.html` and `meta.json` exist.\n187: 2. `meta.json` parses as JSON and matches the schema above.\n188: 3. `index.html` parses, has exactly one `<canvas id=\"game\">`, exactly one `<button id=\"restart\">` after it, and the `<header>` text matches `meta.json`.\n189: 4. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`. ```js
// === CONFIG =============================================================
// Canvas dimensions
const CANVAS_WIDTH = 800;
const CANVAS_HEIGHT = 600;

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
let loopId = null;

// Placeholder game state
let phase = 'menu'; // 'menu' | 'playing' | 'gameover'
let score = 0;

// === RESET ==============================================================
function reset() {
  // Reset state variables
  phase = 'playing';
  score = 0;
  // start loop
  startLoop();
}

// === LOOP ==============================================================
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  let lastTime = performance.now();
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // placeholder update/render
    } catch (e) {
      console.error('loop error', e);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// === INPUT ==============================================================
document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') reset();
});
restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);

// === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: phase,
    score,
    player: { x: 0, y: 0, visible: true },
    objective: 'Tap to cycle lanes and avoid obstacles.',
  };
}

// === BOOT ==============================================================
reset();
```

But we need to call publishState each frame. Could add call inside tick try block at end: `publishState();` This will keep .__game defined each tick. But if not defined before first frame, maybe not needed but better to define initial state.

function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    function tick(now) {
        try {
            const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
            lastTime = now;
            // Clear canvas
            ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
            update(dt);
            render();
            publishState();
        } catch (e) {
            console.error('loop error', e);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

```js
// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  // Update player position based on lane
  player.y = player.lane * LANE_HEIGHT + LANE_HEIGHT / 2;

  // Spawn hazards
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = MIN_SPAWN_INTERVAL + Math.random() * (MAX_SPAWN_INTERVAL - MIN_SPAWN_INTERVAL);
  }

  // Move hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x -= hazardSpeed * dt;
    // Remove off-screen hazards
    if (h.x + h.width < 0) hazards.splice(i, 1);
  }

  // Collision detection
  for (const h of hazards) {
    if (h.x <= player.x && h.x + h.width >= player.x && player.lane !== h.gapLane) {
      gameOver();
      break;
    }
  }
}

// === RENDER ==============================================================
function render() {
  // Clear background
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_WIDTH, CANVAS_HEIGHT);

  // Draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ff0055';
  for (const h of hazards) {
    // Top block
    if (h.gapLane > 0) {
      ctx.fillRect(h.x, 0, h.width, h.gapLane * LANE_HEIGHT);
    }
    // Bottom block
    const bottomY = (h.gapLane + 1) * LANE_HEIGHT;
    const bottomHeight = CANVAS_HEIGHT - bottomY;
    if (bottomHeight > 0) {
      ctx.fillRect(h.x, bottomY, h.width, bottomHeight);
    }
  }

  // Draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, 8, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();

  // Draw HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText('Score ' + score, 12, 22);
}
```

```js
  // === UPDATE ==============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    // Update player Y based on lane
    player.y = player.lane * LANE_HEIGHT + LANE_HEIGHT / 2;

    // Update spawn timer
    spawnTimer -= dt;
    if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
      spawnHazard();
      spawnTimer = MIN_SPAWN_INTERVAL + Math.random() * (MAX_SPAWN_INTERVAL - MIN_SPAWN_INTERVAL);
    }

    // Move hazards
    for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const h = hazards[i];
      h.x -= hazardSpeed * dt;
      if (h.x + h.width < 0) {
        hazards.splice(i, 1);
        score++; // Increment score for each passed hazard
      }
    }

    // Collision detection
    for (const h of hazards) {
      if (h.x <= player.x && h.x + h.width >= player.x && player.lane !== h.gapLane) {
        gameOver();
        break;
      }
    }
  }

  // === RENDER ==============================================================
  function render() {
    // Clear canvas
    ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_WIDTH, CANVAS_HEIGHT);

    // Draw hazards
    ctx.fillStyle = '#ff0055';
    for (const h of hazards) {
      // Top solid part
      if (h.gapLane > 0) {
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, 0, h.width, h.gapLane * LANE_HEIGHT);
      }
      // Bottom solid part
      const bottomY = (h.gapLane + 1) * LANE_HEIGHT;
      const bottomHeight = CANVAS_HEIGHT - bottomY;
      if (bottomHeight > 0) {
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, bottomY, h.width, bottomHeight);
      }
    }

    // Draw player
    ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, 8, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // Draw HUD - score
    ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
    ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.textAlign = 'left';
    ctx.fillText('Score ' + score, 12, 22);
  }
```

```js
  // === HELPERS ==============================================================
  function cycleLane() {
    player.lane = (player.lane + 1) % LANE_COUNT;
  }

  function spawnHazard() {
    const gapLane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
    const hazard = {
      x: CANVAS_WIDTH,
      width: HAZARD_WIDTH,
      gapLane: gapLane,
    };
    hazards.push(hazard);
  }

  function gameOver() {
    phase = 'gameover';
    // Stop loop
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    loopId = null;
  }
```

```js
// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  // TODO: update game logic (player movement, hazards, collisions, scoring)
  // For now, ensure player position reflects current lane
  // Lane positions: 0=top,1=middle,2=bottom
  const laneWidth = CANVAS_WIDTH / 3;
  player.x = player.lane * laneWidth + laneWidth / 2;
  player.y = CANVAS_HEIGHT * 0.75; // position near bottom
}

// === RENDER ==============================================================
function render() {
  // Clear canvas
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, CANVAS_WIDTH, CANVAS_HEIGHT);
  
  // Draw hazards (as filled rectangles in lanes)
  const laneWidth = CANVAS_WIDTH / 3;
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ff00aa';
  for (const h of hazards) {
    // Expected hazard format: { lane: 0|1|2, y: number, height: number }
    const hx = h.lane * laneWidth + laneWidth / 2;
    const hy = h.y;
    const hHeight = h.height || 20;
    const hWidth = laneWidth - 20;
    ctx.fillRect(hx - hWidth / 2, hy, hWidth, hHeight);
  }
  
  // Draw player dot
  ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, 8, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  
  // HUD - score
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
}
```

Implementation:

function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "square";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.1, audioCtx.currentTime);
    // Ramp down volume to avoid click
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("beep error", e);
  }
}

Add at top audioCtx variable.

// === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = 'square';
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // ignore audio errors (e.g., autoplay restrictions)
  }
}

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Lane Cycler
Concept: Dot auto‑advances through a three‑lane tunnel; each tap cycles the dot to the next lane (top→middle→bottom→top), and obstacles have gaps only in specific lanes, demanding precise timing of lane changes.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.

It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:lane-cycler:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically.
